  254: Tauberian orbit law, the totient evaluation of the critical equation, and the
  255: finite-size consequences are the refinements used here.
  256: 
  257: Let \(\mathbf X_m\) be uniformly distributed on \(X_m\), and put
  258: \[
  259:  Y_m=m^{-1}\log d_m(\mathbf X_m).
  260: \]
  261: 
  262: \begin{theoremG}[Full large deviations across the freezing corner]
  263: The sequence \((Y_m)\) satisfies a large deviation principle with speed
  264: \(m\) and good rate function
  265: \[
  266:  I(\alpha)=\sup_{t\in\RR}\{t\alpha-P(t)+\log\varphi\}.
  267: \]
  268: Its effective domain is \([0,\alpha_+]\), and
  269: \[
  270:  I(\alpha)=\log\varphi-\sigma_0\alpha
  271:  \qquad(0\le\alpha\le v_c).
  272: \]
  273: For every \(0<\alpha<v_c\), the probabilities of shrinking neighborhoods
  274: have the exact exponential rate \(-I(\alpha)\).
  275: \end{theoremG}
  276: 
  277: The complete statement and proof are given in \Cref{thm:full-fiber-ldp}.
  278: The new orbit-filling step fills both parities of every sufficiently large
  279: target layer while preserving the source multiplicity.
  280: 
  281: For a fixed finite set of primes, \Cref{prop:prime-support-interface} gives
  282: the exact joint generating function for multiplier exponents and generator
  283: cost and transfers it to \(C_m(k)\) with the active boundary weights
  284: \(4,3,1\).  We do not assert a directional coefficient or quenched-velocity
  285: asymptotic: the needed parameter-uniform smooth-point estimate and uniform
  286: integrability for the unbounded cost are not supplied by the cited ACSV
  287: results.  \Cref{rem:prime-support-local-open-interface} records the exact
  288: remaining analytic input.
  289: 
  290: The proof of \Cref{thm:negative-temperature-freezing} includes a separate
  291: layer-count lemma transferring Weinstein's convention \(f_j=F_{j+1}\).  It
  292: distinguishes the levels \(k=1,2\), endpoints, early layers, and the eventual
  293: equality asserted in Weinstein's theorem.  The compact-operator conclusion is
  294: stated precisely in \Cref{prop:no-analytic-compact-representation} and follows
  295: from analytic perturbation theory.
  296: 
  297: The pressure corner prevents a direct full application of the
  298: G\"artner--Ellis theorem: its lower-bound conclusion does not cover slopes
  299: strictly between the one-sided derivatives at \(-\sigma_0\).  The
  300: orbit-filling argument in \Cref{thm:full-fiber-ldp} supplies those missing
  301: local lower bounds and proves the full large deviation principle, with an
  302: affine rate on the coexistence interval.
  303: 
  304: \subsection{Contribution boundary}
  305: 
  306: For auditability, the dependency boundary is summarized before the detailed
  307: discussion.
  308: \noindent\textbf{Koc\'abov\'a--Mas\'akov\'a--Pelantov\'a.}
  309: Imported: extremal values and maximizing integers for the ordinary Fibonacci
  310: partition function.  Proved here from that input: exact convention and
  311: interval transfer to all maximizing finite-window residues.
  312: 
  313: \noindent\textbf{Weinstein.}
  314: Imported: the free generator monoid, multiplier product, orbit decomposition,
  315: and eventual layer stabilization.  Proved here from that input: the uniform
  316: early-layer bound, weighted cost renewal, exact finite-window transfer, and
  317: the prime-support coefficient interface.
  318: 
  319: \noindent\textbf{Erd\H{o}s measure and Bernoulli convolutions.}
  320: Sidorov--Vershik identify normalized finite Fibonacci representation
  321: frequencies with approximants to the Erd\H{o}s measure
  322: \cite{SidorovVershik1998}.  Hu studied its golden-ratio local dimensions
  323: \cite{Hu1997}; Lau--Ngai gave the exact \(L^q\)-spectrum for \(q>0\)
  324: \cite{LauNgai1998}; Feng--Olivier and Feng gave the complete real spectrum and
  325: the negative first-order transition \cite{FengOlivier2003,Feng2005}.  These
  326: are imported results, not merely related background.
  327: 
  328: If \(\mu\) denotes the golden-ratio Bernoulli convolution and
  329: \(\tau_\mu(t)\) uses the convention
  330: \(\tau_\mu(t)=\lim \log\sum_I\mu(I)^t/\log|I|\), then the level masses are
